## catalogue-profile / limitations

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/limitations/en -->
Taxonomic and source-bounded group introduction. Historical source subsets are not assumed equivalent to the entire current group; unrepresented biological evidence remains to be reviewed. This reader is not externally expert-reviewed.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/0/sectionEvidence/0/evidenceSummary/en -->
The 2025 study proposes Cryptomantis, with prolatus as its type species and two supported species-group clades. It includes nine described names, counting newly described paganus, plus five unconfirmed candidate lineages. The study explicitly lacks identified morphological synapomorphies; variable folds alone do not diagnose the entire clade. The links select eight fixed accepted species within those groups. Paganus lacks an exact pinned name and remains in a separate pending record. Neither candidates nor a proposed rank silently change catalogue identities.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/0/sectionEvidence/1/evidenceSummary/en -->
The study adds a burtoniorum holotype sequence and topotypic references for several names to distinguish earlier broad labels from narrower lineages. It targets 16S, 12S, COI and nuclear RAG1 using Sanger and nanopore amplicon methods. Its candidate thresholds vary, and some vouchers lack adequate morphology. High bootstrap support is not proof of reproductive isolation or a universal rank criterion. Mountain and river isolation remains a proposed explanation, not a directly measured gene-flow barrier.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/0/sectionEvidence/2/evidenceSummary/en -->
The paganus account describes two Ecuadorian cloud-forest localities, night-time litter and vegetation observations, and a gravid female. It does not establish observed egg deposition, full development, diet or genus-wide ecological requirements. Repeated paratype numbers across sex/age categories and differing holotype observation/collection dates remain unresolved in the pending source record. The authors’ Near Threatened proposal is dated interpretation; encounter frequency is not a census and protected-area presence is not a demographic test.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/1/sectionEvidence/0/evidenceSummary/en -->
In the Tena study, variabilis has a single visual record and malkini two observations at one river site. In contrast, acerus, eriphus and mallii are mountain source taxa assigned from downstream water without a local visual observation. The source treats those mountain signals as false positives for living-organism presence at the monitored sites. Its unnamed Pristimantis sp. (INABIO 15591) remains outside the selected accepted-species links. Combining these histories does not turn molecular transport into a low-elevation range extension.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/1/sectionEvidence/1/evidenceSummary/en -->
Short 16S matching, negative controls and a regional reference database support interpretation of the molecular units, but do not themselves locate the animals that shed DNA. Upstream transport is proposed, without tracing particular frogs or molecules. The inconsistent monitoring elevations in Study site and Discussion remain unresolved. Neither a negative water test nor a fitted visual detection probability certifies basin-wide absence, population size or an activity cycle for every Pristimantis.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/2/sectionEvidence/0/evidenceSummary/en -->
The retained Pristimantis subset contains 3 source-labelled workbook rows linked to 3 distinct accepted species pages. At the row level, 3 are coded direct-developing, 3 terrestrial-ovipositing, and juvenile habitat flags mark 0 aquatic, 3 terrestrial and 0 semiterrestrial entries. These are counts of source rows, not distinct accepted species prevalence or a complete genus census. Historical names sharing a destination retain separate trait statements. Terrestrial eggs do not automatically imply direct development; juvenile habitat is distinct from adult preference. The pages preserve multiple egg-site flags and original upstream reference indices without claiming independent observation of every trait.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/2/sectionEvidence/1/evidenceSummary/en -->
In this source-row subset, 3 head-width entries and 3 tibia-length entries are explicitly inferred. Unflagged lengths are not independently certified measurements. Mixed means/maxima, missing explicit header units and inconsistent sex/stage labels prevent a uniform diagnostic size series. The full article models 766 communities and reports 36% explained variation in direct-development proportions; functional diversity is a proxy and the fitted associations do not establish competition, predation or physiological thresholds for each linked species. Across the full workbook, 463 rows now have retained reader destinations and one unresolved source spelling remains outside the routes; those 463 rows represent 457 distinct accepted destinations, not a revised biological census.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/3/sectionEvidence/0/evidenceSummary/en -->
The selected Pristimantis subset contains 17 nominal species rows and 230 cumulative records from the Fátima parish study in Pastaza, Ecuador, published in 2026 from September 2013–September 2018 monitoring. These are source-attributed records, not a current provincial census, independently certified distinct animals or per-area densities. Day/night visual and photographic encounters, quadrats, transects and pitfall traps span six local habitats. The 900–1,100 m site gradient does not establish each species’ complete elevation range, and later weather summaries do not establish physiological tolerance.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/3/sectionEvidence/1/evidenceSummary/en -->
Species readers retain the individual status-column assignments and specific habitat rank statements where the Results provide them. These dated source categories are not newly verified current IUCN assessments or quantitative local extinction probabilities. Dominance in a forest or infrastructure sample does not prove universal habitat preference, a causal benefit of disturbance or population recovery. General threatened-genus wording for Atelopus and Hyloscirtus does not overwrite the LC entries for spumarius and phyllognathus in Table 2. No disease prevalence, breeding experiment or tested thermal mechanism was established by this inventory.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/3/sectionEvidence/2/evidenceSummary/en -->
Global coverage 97.4% describes the sampled assemblage rather than the proportion of all provincial species found. High habitat coverage does not eliminate undetected rare species. The Methods’ next-record wording, per-habitat effort totals and Simpson-index interpretation remain unreconciled. Results enumerate seventy-five mixed taxon rows, while Discussion says eighty-five species; the printed conservation footer does not sum to the 614 total records. Those disagreements are retained without a guessed correction. Year-five diversity changes and Jaccard turnover are community observations, not causal climate responses or trends of every linked species.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/3/sectionEvidence/3/evidenceSummary/en -->
Methods explicitly describe genus-only Pristimantis identification without molecular analyses, whereas Table 2 prints numerous named species plus five separate Pristimantis sp. rows totaling twenty-six records. Named species routes preserve attributed statements and flag that conflict, rather than certify vouchers or diagnoses. The five unnamed rows stay outside accepted-species routes and are not pooled into any named species. Original photographs, vouchers, diagnostic evidence or author clarification are needed to resolve the source identities.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/4/sectionEvidence/0/evidenceSummary/en -->
The selected Pristimantis routes retain 4 species and 49 reported adult records from nocturnal sampling in Tolima, Colombia, during September–December 2018. Species pages separate each site/elevation and forest/open row, with body, substrate and air temperatures in degrees Celsius as mean ± standard deviation and observed range. Body temperature means dorsal skin at the encounter; nearby air is measured twenty centimetres above the frog. These are observed temperatures, not critical limits, preferred temperatures or a complete physiological niche for the genus. The study does not establish all-season exposure or independently marked unique individuals.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/4/sectionEvidence/1/evidenceSummary/en -->
A nine-animal/four-species repeated skin–cloacal comparison is used to assume that dorsal skin represents internal temperature; nonsignificant differences do not independently certify equivalence in every sampled species and environment. Six hourly ground loggers cover three elevations and two broad canopy categories, but are not positioned at every frog’s substrate. Forest canopy above sixty percent and open canopy up to forty percent exclude intermediate canopy conditions from those definitions. The pooled analyses adjust for body size without a phylogenetic model, so species composition and habitat choice limit claims about evolutionary causation.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/4/sectionEvidence/2/evidenceSummary/en -->
The source finds stronger pooled body–substrate than body–air associations and proposes conductive heat exchange, but does not measure heat flux, water loss or species-specific temperature choice. Open/forest mean differences are significant at low/middle elevations and nonsignificant at the highest; habitat variance is also nonsignificant. Table 2 prints p < 0.000 and a low-open body–air p > 0.789 despite prose saying significance in all cases; those entries are not silently corrected. Discussion also calls selected same-elevation species temperatures similar although their printed means differ. Forest specialization and lower tolerance are proposed future tests rather than established warming vulnerability or conservation outcomes.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/5/sectionEvidence/0/evidenceSummary/en -->
The selected Pristimantis subset has 1 Guayaquil checklist rows: 1 record conservation areas, 1 unprotected native remnants and 0 urban parks. Categories overlap and are presence assignments, not summed unique populations or densities. The source combines fieldwork from 2008–2025, museums, databases and screened secondary records, including selected near-boundary sites. These routes do not represent uniform coverage of every urban habitat or current occurrence throughout all Guayaquil territory. Detailed specimen appendices and photographs have not been independently retrieved and checked.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/5/sectionEvidence/1/evidenceSummary/en -->
Within this subset, 0 source rows are marked endemic to Ecuador and 0 identify Guayaquil as the type locality. Neither flag means city-only distribution or verifies all present populations at a historical type site. The article’s taxonomy follows 2025 source frameworks, while these reader routes use exact-name candidates in frozen COL26.8. Same-name matching does not independently certify original vouchers or biological concept equivalence. Overall type-locality totals differ between tables and Discussion, and the discrepancy is retained without a guessed correction.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/5/sectionEvidence/2/evidenceSummary/en -->
Global IUCN 2025 and Ecuadorian 2021 amphibian categories are retained as dated source assignments with different geographic scales. Presence in a conservation category does not prove effective protection, and urban records do not experimentally demonstrate pollution tolerance, reproductive rate or inherited adaptation. Discussion’s cited Bd reports are not new prevalence measurements; caecilian rediscovery is not a population estimate, and introduced bullfrog records are not measured local impact. GIS land-cover change and biological persistence describe different evidence streams. Restoration, invasive-species monitoring and education recommendations remain proposed actions.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/6/sectionEvidence/0/evidenceSummary/en -->
The selected Pristimantis routes (Pristimantis kuri, Pristimantis muricatus, Pristimantis nyctophylax, Pristimantis walkeri) retain individual encounters, vouchers and elevations from western Ecuador. The source’s provincial additions and distances between known records describe changes in documented knowledge, not observed colonization, continuous occupancy or a measured dispersal rate. Non-systematic searches do not establish absence at unvisited sites. Historical cited elevation ranges are distinct from lower or higher new records, and precise encounter elevations must not be substituted with camp elevations.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/6/sectionEvidence/1/evidenceSummary/en -->
The linked pages separate males, females, juveniles and sex-undetermined animals rather than using every length as an adult range. Perching near streams or puddles is not proof of egg placement, larval habitat or quantified habitat preference. Amplexus and vocalization remain particular reported events, without inferred recruitment or acoustic parameters. Methods’2014–2023 window differs from a2008 and a2024 account record; printed two-decimal lengths are not certified finer precision than the stated0.1 mm method. Cited identification descriptions and dated threat categories are not independently refreshed specimen diagnoses or risk assessments.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/7/sectionEvidence/0/evidenceSummary/en -->
The Pristimantis subset of S3 Table contains 3 source rows. 3 names match frozen COL26.8 species exactly and have selected reading routes. 0 rows remain unresolved. Qualifiers cf., aff., gr. and sp. are retained as printed, without treating similarity, a species group or an unidentified specimen as an accepted species. The full inventory’s99 amphibian concepts include such unresolved labels, so99 is not the number of individually certified accepted-species pages. Exact matching also does not independently verify museum vouchers or biological concept equivalence.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/7/sectionEvidence/1/evidenceSummary/en -->
This subset has 1 rows in Conceição do Araguaia, 2 in Carajás Mosaic, 1 in Ourilândia do Norte/São Félix do Xingu and 2 in São Geraldo do Araguaia. Fieldwork code1 occurs in 1, 0, 1 and 2 rows respectively. These derived counts include any unresolved labels above and count presence/source assignments, not individuals or distinct populations; area and evidence categories overlap. S5 effort shows no direct Carajás expedition by this project, with other areas sampled unequally. Museum and literature occurrence cannot automatically be dated to the2021–2022 fieldwork, and blank cells are not demonstrated absence.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/7/sectionEvidence/2/evidenceSummary/en -->
F, O and Pa describe compiled forest, open-vegetation and perianthropic habitat labels. They do not measure preferences, pollution tolerance, reproduction or mining impact for all members of this genus. IUCN entries are dated source assignments with no per-row assessment date, including retained dash values. Regional sequence totals and marker-delimited groups do not certify a barcode or genetic diagnosis for every listed row. The selected species pages retain their individual source cells and any earlier treatment evidence; unexamined workbook, tree, call and specimen data are not presented as independently verified.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/7/sectionEvidence/3/evidenceSummary/en -->
S3 File Table1 has 28 Pristimantis specimen rows under 3 literal source labels. It marks16S sequences in26, COI in27, and both in25 rows. These are marker-availability counts for distinct MPEG accession entries, not total living populations, genome coverage or every species in the genus. 0 unresolved labels remain absent from this genus subset; they are not assigned to accepted species. The source worksheets do not supply per-row collection dates, coordinates, life stages or sequence strings. Existing regional inventory routes also include nonsequenced species, and presence in the occurrence table does not imply barcode availability.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/7/sectionEvidence/4/evidenceSummary/en -->
The Pristimantis subset prints Correlation16S-COI True in1, False in24 and NA in3 rows. The original labels are not interpreted as an r coefficient or specimen identification success. ASAP group IDs are marker-local; differing numbers across genes do not alone establish differing partitions. Species pages record actual within-name groups and any group sharing with other labels. Shared clusters do not merge species, and splits do not formally describe new taxa or prove hybridization/reproductive isolation. Mitochondrial marker comparisons need independent morphology, nuclear data and sampling context for broader taxonomic conclusions; those were not independently reconstructed here.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/8/sectionEvidence/0/evidenceSummary/en -->
The 2025 bibliographic review provides named host entries linked here for Pristimantis shrevei, Pristimantis lanthanites, Pristimantis altamazonicus, Pristimantis conspicillatus, Pristimantis diadematus, Pristimantis fenestratus, Pristimantis variabilis. Across the selected names other than expressly broad complexes, it lists Oswaldocruzia brevispicula, Oswaldocruzia cassonei, Oswaldocruzia lopesi, Oswaldocruzia mazzai, Oswaldocruzia tcheprakovae, Oswaldocruzia touzeti. The regional literature covers Caribbean, Panamanian and Neotropical areas, with host-specific reported places retained in the linked species accounts. These entries were not animals newly sampled together and do not establish genus-wide prevalence, pathogenicity, host specificity, ancestral ecology or comparative susceptibility. Missing host records do not establish parasite absence.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/8/sectionEvidence/1/evidenceSummary/en -->
Links follow exact written host names in the frozen COL26.8 catalogue, without independently authenticating historical host specimens.  This selected genus subset supplies no additional unmatched host label in the extracted host/locality rows. Parasite synonymy and historical host reinterpretations are the review's treatment of earlier literature, not a new specimen or sequence reassessment.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/8/sectionEvidence/2/evidenceSummary/en -->
The authors surveyed published regional literature from 1819–2025 across seven databases and monographs, excluding incomplete records, unpublished theses and meeting material, and parasites not identified to species. Database-specific query strings and search dates are not given in the inspected methods. Overall host totals and some account summaries disagree internally; no corrected host census is inferred here. Life-cycle studies cited in the discussion concern O. filiformis and O. pipiens, and cannot establish the infection route of each regional molineid. Habitat, host size, skin exposure and cospeciation are discussed as possible explanations alongside uneven collection and study effort, rather than demonstrated mechanisms for these genus members. Supplementary Table S1, original vouchers, cited papers and deposited sequences have not been independently reviewed here.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/9/sectionEvidence/0/evidenceSummary/en -->
The2023 study retains these16 named members in its revised myersi group: Pristimantis festae, Pristimantis floridus, Pristimantis gladiator, Pristimantis gralarias, Pristimantis hectus, Pristimantis leoni, Pristimantis lucidosignatus, Pristimantis munozi, Pristimantis myersi, Pristimantis ocreatus, Pristimantis onorei, Pristimantis pyrrhomerus, Pristimantis repens, Pristimantis scopaeus, Pristimantis sirnigeli, Pristimantis xeniolum. It proposes Trachyphrynus as a formal subgenus name for that restricted clade. Albujai, bicantus, nelsongalloi andsambalan are excluded by the inferred relationships; jubatus andthe verecundus/celator/mutabilis clade are also outside its narrower definition. Those decisions concern this study's supraspecific hypothesis, not a revised diagnosis of all Pristimantis or an independently authenticated current catalogue. Floridus, repens, scopaeus andxeniolum were retained without sampled phylogenetic placement.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/9/sectionEvidence/1/evidenceSummary/en -->
The analysis uses mitochondrial16S, model-based trees and uncorrected p-distance, with2% as a candidate-screening heuristic. The authors distinguish confirmed candidates, unconfirmed candidates and deep conspecific lineages; these are research categories rather than automatically accepted scientific species. Some candidate comparisons overlap below2%, and sp.13 is UCC in Results butCCS in Discussion. Mitochondrial divergence and reciprocal monophyly do not alone demonstrate nuclear independence or reproductive isolation. Methods760/219 versusanalysis742/215terminal counts remain separate; ultrafast bootstrap and Bayesian posterior support are different statistics.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/9/sectionEvidence/2/evidenceSummary/en -->
The restricted group definition includes short snouts, robust bodies, FingerI shorter thanII, ToeV longer thanIII and no cranial crests. Female15.8–34.6mm andmale12.6–20.5mm SVL are pooled group-level ranges, not fresh measurements for every linked species. Generally narrow discs have a floridus exception, and that species remains unsampled. The paper acknowledges no unambiguous phenotypic synapomorphy; resemblance alone cannot assign all superficially similar frogs to this clade. The morphology and low-perching/ground-use statements apply to the source’s defined group, not the entire genus.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/9/sectionEvidence/3/evidenceSummary/en -->
Sixteen GenBank sequence labels are revised in Table1. Those are sequence-specific decisions, not formal synonymy for every original species or blanket transfer of all historic localities. Table2 keeps previous distributions and Thisstudy restrictions separate. Hectus lacks type-locality DNA and its sampling distance is reported as100km in Results versus45km in Discussion. Suggested valley or river-basin barriers and cryptic-trait retention are hypotheses, without a direct gene-flow or selection experiment. The linked pages keep those limits and distinct geographic comparisons visible.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/9/sectionEvidence/4/evidenceSummary/en -->
The paper says16recognized species plus8candidates would become23; that arithmetic is not silently repaired or accepted as a certified total. It also labels a western-montane list nine species while enumerating ten. Table2 historical IUCN andEcuador2021 labels are distinct from current assessments, and reassessment requests do not measure population decline. Earlier recorded downloads failed; both DOCX files have now been retrieved through the official PMC cloud. All Appendix row cells were read, while remaining distance values, raw aligned sequences, plotted pixels and original specimens remain independently unreviewed. The genus reading route explains this selected group study, not complete biology for the genus.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/9/sectionEvidence/5/evidenceSummary/en -->
The linked comparisons add Pristimantis albujai, Pristimantis bicantus, Pristimantis nelsongalloi, Pristimantis sambalan, Pristimantis caprifer, Pristimantis acuminatus, Pristimantis eriphus, Pristimantis celator, Pristimantis mutabilis, Pristimantis verecundus, Pristimantis jubatus. Albujai has alternative group placements; bicantus, nelsongalloi, sambalan andcaprifer occur in a branch outside the restricted myersi definition, near the acuminatus/eriphus branch. The verecundus/celator/mutabilis andjubatus branches are also outside that narrower definition. Sampled bicantus paraphyly and proposed euphronides allocation are source-specific hypotheses, not completed synonymization or evidence of a particular hybridization history.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/9/sectionEvidence/6/evidenceSummary/en -->
The Discussion contrasts expanded discs and a discoidal fold in formerly included verecundus/jubatus branches with the narrower myersi definition. It separately notes absent vocal slits in celator andjubatus. These are literature-informed contrasts, not new measurements of every specimen; absent vocal slits are not proof of absent calling. The referenced arboreal versus low-perching comparison is not a climbing-performance experiment or a habitat census for the entire genus.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/9/sectionEvidence/7/evidenceSummary/en -->
The wider verecundus clade includes nine unconfirmed candidates alongside three named members; old verecundus labels relabelled sp.2/sp.10 are not transferred back to accepted verecundus. The jubatus sister candidate sp.1 includes old hectus-labelled Colombian specimens, whose locality is not automatically a new jubatus locality. Carchi andPichincha named-sample contexts remain separate from candidate localities and complete native ranges. Additional morphology/bioacoustics is requested by the authors, and Appendix vouchers/distances remain independently unreviewed.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/9/sectionEvidence/8/evidenceSummary/en -->
The retrieved Appendix has231rows,of which155match43exact accepted-name labels after expanding the explicit P.abbreviation. The remaining76rows retain sp./cf./unresolved-name labels rather than become accepted species. “Our study” and “Genbank” remain printed provenance categories. Floridus has four specimen/locality rows butno GenBank accessions,so it remains unsampled for molecular placement in this study. Matching a donor/accession does not certify reproductive isolation, current classification ororiginal museum identity.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/9/sectionEvidence/9/evidenceSummary/en -->
Each donor retains locality,latitude,longitude andelevation fields with row-level locators. Onorei Toisán rows174–176 print N in longitude fields;mutabilis row19 prints12O2 with letterO; decimal commas andsigned/hemisphere notation vary. These strings are preserved as reported,without inventing W,1202m orreplacementcoordinates. A country/province entry orblank/dash does not supply a new map point orcomplete current range. Coordinate reconciliation remains separate from reading a printed row.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/9/sectionEvidence/10/evidenceSummary/en -->
Appendix devillei KU217991 atPapallacta andpichincha QCAZ11673 atMojanda both useEF493688.1. That record conflict does not enact synonymy orsettle the original specimen identity. Row219 prints brevifros; the recorded dataset316115 exact-name query found no resolved frozen accepted bridge,so its donor is not silently reassigned to brevifrons oranother name. Qualified bicantus andnumbered candidates retain their own source labels.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/9/sectionEvidence/11/evidenceSummary/en -->
Both DOCX supplements were retrieved from the official PMC cloud with matching DOI, license and MD5 values. All 231 Appendix donor rows, all nine distance-table terminal-label columns and 67 selected numerical cells have now been read. Remaining numerical cells, raw alignments, original sequences, museum vouchers and figure pixels remain unreviewed. Selected routes expose source evidence without certifying complete biology for every Pristimantis.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/9/sectionEvidence/12/evidenceSummary/en -->
The nine supplementary tables report uncorrected 16S percent p-distances. The 67 selected cells read here include leoni–sp. 13 values of 1.3%, 1.5% and 2.1%, and gladiator–festae values of 1.9%, 2.1%, 2.2% and 2.5%. Mutabilis-labelled pairs include 2.4% and 2.5%. Thus both cross-label and within-label comparisons can fall on different sides of the study’s 2% heuristic. These selected cells are neither exhaustive ranges nor independent tests of reproductive isolation; raw alignment and original sequence identity remain unverified.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/9/sectionEvidence/13/evidenceSummary/en -->
Table S4 labels all ten terminals sp. 12. Selected values are 0.5% for Morán DBR406/DHMECN13339, 1.5% and 1.7% for Tufiño–Maldonado DBR264 and DBR263 against Virgen Negra DHMECN13354, 0.4% for DBR263 against Morán DHMECN13339, and 0.4% for DBR263/DBR264. These particular cells are not a complete within-lineage range or evidence assigning sp. 12 to an accepted species. DBR and Appendix ZSFQ codes are not automatically equivalent.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/9/sectionEvidence/14/evidenceSummary/en -->
Table S8 terminal 5 prints “sp. 18_floridus” with DHMECN13336, while retaining the numbered candidate label. Its selected distance to Dayuma QCAZ25589 is 6.0%; TH639 versus this donor is 7.1%, and TH668 versus it is 5.8%. TH668 versus QCAZ25589 is 4.4% and versus TH639 is 5.2%; Morán DHMECN13332 versus QCAZ25589 is 0.2%. The 7.1% cell therefore belongs to TH639/DHMECN13336, not any of these selected TH668 comparisons. Embedded floridus text does not authorize moving these sp. 18 donors into accepted floridus; its four Appendix specimen rows still lack accessions. Sequence-quality and uncertain Dayuma locality questions remain unresolved.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/9/sectionEvidence/15/evidenceSummary/en -->
Table S9 calls Otonga MZUTI2063 and MZUTI2092 onorei; the Appendix calls them lucidosignatus. Their selected mutual distance is 0.1%; comparisons with Toisán MZUTI4241 are 0.9% and 0.7%, and 2092 against Las Gralarias MZUTI2023 is 1.3%. These shared-code source comparisons do not certify a whole-species synonym. MZUTI1465 is present in S9 but not the corresponding Appendix donor list; Table S2 prints 219_027 versus Appendix 2019 027; S7 QCAZ17950 locality is Páramo Guamaní versus Appendix Papallacta. Preserve these differences without inventing donor substitutions or new geographic points.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/9/sectionEvidence/16/evidenceSummary/en -->
The reading receipt retains 67 exact row-terminal/column-terminal addresses, both original labels, printed percent strings and the DOCX SHA-256. All nine terminal-label columns have been read; other numerical matrix cells remain unreviewed. S7 has 36 terminal rows but a blank final numbered header; S9 has 39 terminal rows with the same final-header issue. Selected column addresses were taken only from explicit printed numbered headers. These header anomalies do not justify rescaling values, inventing a terminal or certifying every table cell.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/10/sectionEvidence/0/evidenceSummary/en -->
These initial routes cover three supplementary local accounts, while the revision’s focal descriptions and redescriptions remain to be read in detail. Atratus is reported from eastern-slope upper montane forest and placed in a supported phoxocephalus subgroup; galdi is a lower montane encounter and a phylogenetic outgroup; lymani is a western-slope and pastureland encounter characterized locally as terrestrial. Their evidence roles and local intervals are not interchangeable.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/10/sectionEvidence/1/evidenceSummary/en -->
The2016–2020 work covered97days with visual and auditory searches across heterogeneous habitats and methods. Common, uncommon and rare describe occurrence on50–100%,25–50% and less than25% of sampling days/nights in suitable habitat; the authors could not calculate standardized survey effort or relative abundance. The approximately50km² core site is distinct from the almost100km² psychrophila search. Regional land-use, exotic-species and possible disease concerns are not causal tests for every Pristimantis species.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/10/sectionEvidence/2/evidenceSummary/en -->
The concatenated analyses use mitochondrial12S/16S and nuclearRAG-1 with maximum-likelihood and Bayesian inference. The source places atratus with percultus, chomskyi, andinogigas and multicolor, while discussing unresolved boundaries between chomskyi andandinogigas. Approximate genetic similarity and personal field observations motivate further revision; they do not enact a synonymy here. A printed moderate-support Bayesian upper value9.95 is retained as a source inconsistency rather than accepted as a possible probability.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/10/sectionEvidence/3/evidenceSummary/en -->
The revision explicitly suspects that Abra de Zamora andinognomus differs from its Tapichalaca type-locality population, despite frequent local calls and historical paratype citations. Vidua’s historical all-female hypothesis is revised by observed males, while encounter differences could reflect sex-specific detectability. Neither a catalogue-name match nor etymology resolves population identity or reproductive mode.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/10/sectionEvidence/4/evidenceSummary/en -->
The source recovers the orestes group with bootstrap100% and posterior1, distinguishing orestes, simonbolivari and bromeliad-associated colodactylus subgroups. Vidua belongs to the first, samaniegoi to the second, and colodactylus/matildae to the third. Strong broad subgroup support does not make every internal placement resolved: vidua’s exact position and samaniegoi’s closest relationships remain uncertain. Candidate lineages remain unnamed and unassigned.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/10/sectionEvidence/5/evidenceSummary/en -->
Vidua and samaniegoi have measured clicking calls from three and five males respectively. Call duration, frequency measurements and numbers of males are separate sampling units. Colodactylus and matildae are associated with terrestrial and arboreal bromeliads and show water-retreat behavior; their advertisement calls are unknown. Short-digit adaptation is a hypothesis rather than a measured adhesion experiment, and absence of heard calling is not inability to vocalize.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/10/sectionEvidence/6/evidenceSummary/en -->
The authors retain Endangered for vidua, propose Critically Endangered for the restricted colodactylus concept, and propose Near Threatened for samaniegoi and matildae. Each interpretation combines its own reported range and habitat context. Localities, area estimates and categories are not interchangeable, independently reconstructed here or verified as current assessments. Matildae’s summarized3360m limit and a3361m paratype elevation remain separately recorded.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/10/sectionEvidence/7/evidenceSummary/en -->
The revision distinguishes cryptomelas concealed black surfaces with pale/orange margins, percultus pustulate dorsal/flank skin, balionotus dorsal flecking, and highly variable versicolor colors. Table3 keeps sex-specific samples and marks some percultus/balionotus measurements as taken from original descriptions. Female body mass includes eggs. Size ranges and coloration belong to their named samples and do not become genus-wide thresholds or independently measured mechanisms.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/10/sectionEvidence/8/evidenceSummary/en -->
Balionotus whistles usually contain one or two notes, occasionally up to eight; its short-call summary and double-note duration are kept distinct. Versicolor has observed and measured chirp-like calling despite males lacking vocal sac/slits, with one to five notes. Cryptomelas and percultus advertisement calls remain unknown in the revision. Per-male sample sizes, counts of calls and frequency measurements are separate units; proposed social triggers are not controlled experiments.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/10/sectionEvidence/9/evidenceSummary/en -->
Huicundomantis is strongly supported broadly, but cryptomelas-group ML support is moderate and the cryptomelas/spinosus relation weak or moderate depending on method. Percultus and balionotus internal relationships remain unresolved. Southern versicolor populations about2%16S different from northern/type-area samples require further revision. No unnamed candidate or geographically distant uncertain record is automatically assigned to a current accepted species; dated conservation proposals remain source interpretations.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/10/sectionEvidence/10/evidenceSummary/en -->
SupplementaryS5 explicitly records selected Pristimantis labels, slope/crest location, endemic-to-Abra/nearby yes/no fields, an IUCN code and encounter within the source’s last ten years. Each route supplies its row/cell locator. These2020 fields are not current assessments or an exhaustive global endemism search; main-text conservation proposals and qualified population identities are retained separately. The inventory has29labels across six source families, including aff./sp. concepts that remain unassigned to accepted nominal species. Source-family terminology and the spelling “Noblela” are not silently used to change catalogue classification.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/10/sectionEvidence/11/evidenceSummary/en -->
S4 gives separate samaniegoi summaries excluding and including the single-note LS110725 record. Table2 uses the exclusion-based duration but inclusion-based rate/frequency. Balionotus double-note duration has mean0.355s in both main text and S4, but SD0.110 versus0.012s respectively; the disagreement remains explicit. Recording temperatures, humidity, distance and voucher/no-voucher fields support provenance, not controlled physiological experiments or authenticated original audio. Files were downloaded from PLOS and hash-preserved.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/10/sectionEvidence/12/evidenceSummary/en -->
The supplementaryS2 exact-name routes include 38 accepted Pristimantis labels, within a123-row table of52printed labels. Forty-five labels across Lynchius/Pristimantis match frozen accepted names; flavomaculatus? and six Pristimantis candidate-label categories remain unassigned. Catalogue equality links pages, while original specimen identity and current taxonomic concept require separate evidence. The Yangana question-mark label is not merged into nominal flavomaculatus, and reused comparative records are not automatically new collections.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/10/sectionEvidence/13/evidenceSummary/en -->
Each sequence page supplies S2 voucher,12S/16S/RAG-1 accession strings, locality text and exact row locators. Hyphens indicate an unspecified accession rather than a missing biological gene. The concatenated three-locus protocol does not give every donor three sequenced loci; Cajanuma MUTPL342 has no RAG-1 accession inS2. Repeated codes across columns are not counted as independent assays. Locations of comparative material do not expand the local29-label S5 inventory. Original GenBank records, sequences and museum vouchers remain unauthenticated; Eight selected S3 comparisons have now been read; other matrix values await separate review.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/10/sectionEvidence/14/evidenceSummary/en -->
Seven selected Pristimantis comparisons cover33individual source-cell pairs. Rounded ranges include colodactylus/matildae5.42–5.82%, samaniegoi/tiktik4.79–6.03%, samaniegoi/bambu6.67–7.28%, percultus/chomskyi3.27–3.74%, andinogigas/chomskyi2.27%, balionotus/hampatusami3.84–5.75%, and one andinognomus Cajanuma/Tapichalaca pair1.32%. These are supplied uncorrected16S percentages with exact donor/cell locators; they are not remeasured alignments, complete population ranges or evidence that a fixed cutoff settles each taxon. The andinognomus pair does not authenticate all Abra de Zamora populations.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/10/sectionEvidence/15/evidenceSummary/en -->
S2/S3 disagree on the versicolor voucher code KU21981/KU218096 for EF493389 and on the QCAZ46738 gagliardoi locality Reserva Mazar/Canar_PN_Sangay. A simonbolivari/simonbolivar spelling difference is linked only at shared KU218254/EF493671 record level. These issues do not enact synonyms, establish new accepted spellings or identify replacement coordinates. A matching sequence or voucher string is weaker evidence than independently authenticated specimen identity.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/11/sectionEvidence/0/evidenceSummary/en -->
The2019 glassfrog study lists Pristimantis crenunguis, Pristimantis pteridophilus, Pristimantis scolodiscus at RíoManduriacu Reserve. The paragraph supplies reserve-level names without specimen codes, individual coordinates or an abundance series. This selected source account does not establish full Pristimantis distribution,genus-wide morphology oridentity of unnamed frogs.
<!-- /evo:text -->

## primaryStudyScopes / sectionEvidence / evidenceSummary

<!-- evo:text /records/catalogue-profile/primaryStudyScopes/11/sectionEvidence/1/evidenceSummary/en -->
The source usesCriticallyEndangered forRhaebo olallai/Centrolene ballux andEndangered for the namedPristimantis species. It reports reserve-level mining/logging pressures andmanagement/land/community actions. These remain2019source labels andclaims,not currentIUCN assessments,newspecies-specific causaldecline measurements orverifiedcurrent delivery. The article’s legal/company statements are retained as historical author context rather than present legal conclusions.
<!-- /evo:text -->
